Compiled by PARAS Sdn Bhd from its Planting Advisory (PA) and Agronomy reports. Figures as reported.

HOW TO USE THIS FILE (instructions for the AI assistant)
- Answer questions about this one estate using ONLY the report extracts below.
- Lead with the direct answer, in short numbered points.
- After each figure, cite the report in brackets, e.g. (PA 2/2026) or (Agronomy 1/2026).
- Always state the period a yield figure covers (e.g. Jan–Jun 2026).
- Fertiliser doses in grams per palm. Money in RM.
- If the extracts do not contain the answer, say so plainly. Do not guess.
- Where two reports disagree, give both figures.

REPORT EXTRACTS
SELUANG ESTATE (INTI), PT Lifere Agro Kapuas (PT LAK), Kalimantan, UMB group. Fact pack from PA Report 2/2026 (Planting Advisor Mr. Boey Chee Weng, visit 20-21 August 2026, period reviewed May 2025 to April 2026, financial year FY 2025/26) and Agronomy Report 1/2026 (Senior Agronomist Mr. Lai Hong Heng, visit 18 May 2026). Plantation Director Mr. Tee Tong Heng; Senior Manager Bpk Deni Irawan; Estate Manager Bpk Ismanto Arga (joined 22 June 2022). The estate's financial year runs May to April, so "FY 2025/26" means May 2025 to April 2026. Money as printed (RM).

AREA (PA 2/2026, area statement as at April 2026; no change since last visit): mature 1,116.66 ha (46.99%), immature 129.61 ha (5.45%), total oil palm 1,246.27 ha (52.44%). Non-cultivated: drains 44.01 ha (ex-PLG 27.61), nursery 12.62 ha, building sites and roads 0.52 ha, vacant plantable 295.21 ha, vacant unplantable 750.36 ha; total non-cultivated 1,130.33 ha. Title 2,376.60 ha. The remaining 1.71 ha of villagers' claims is still unplanted because seedlings were not available. PA field details: 146,653 palms, 118 SPH. PA general charges uses 1,116 mature + 117 immature = 1,233 ha (budget 1,278 ha).
Agronomy 1/2026 Table 1 (as at April 2026): 2013 525.25 ha, 2014 8.42, 2015 11.86, 2017 197.23, 2018 162.06 (was 165.41), 2019 223.07, 2020 63.55, 2022 3.97, 2023 6.29, 2024 32.08, 2025 10.85 (was 3.27); total 1,244.63 ha (previously 1,240.40). Young mature palms 51.9% and prime 43.8% of area. 305.19 ha is low-lying and flood-prone (Agro 3.7 says 25.34%, 5.4.10 says 24.52%).

PALM STAND (Agro Table 2, census October 2025): 2013 137 SPH (71,924 palms), 2014 150, 2015 143, 2017 117, 2018 104, 2019 97, 2020 128, 2022 146, 2023 148, 2024 144, 2025 143; average 122 SPH on 1,240.40 ha (151,510 palms). Low stand is a key yield limit. Supply done (Table 3): 2024 6,239 palms (2017 4,112; 2018 1,617; 2019 510), 2025 11,191 palms (2017 4,461; 2018 3,952; 2019 1,700; 2020 1,078); total 17,430. Stands barely changed in records; verify SPH for P17-P19 (repeat). If confirmed at 97-128 SPH, Agro recommends a feasibility study on further infilling now, before canopy closure makes supply ineffective.

LABOUR (PA, April 2026): total 166 workers vs requirement 168 printed (variance printed as (11); the sub-rows add to 178 required). Harvesters/carriers 54 vs 62 (-8), manuring workers 13 vs 16 (-3); spraying workers 23 vs 20 (+3); mandores 11 vs 12; general workers 16 vs 19; other categories at requirement. Nine new harvesters were to be recruited in September 2026, which would make harvesters adequate. Semi-mechanical manuring would cut manuring labour need. PA states worker ratio 1:9 ha vs ideal 1:12.
STAFF (PA): manager, KTU, four division assistants and an Askep (Rafael Raya, from 2 Jan 2026); adequate, no change.

GENERAL CHARGES (PA, 12 months to April 2026): grand total RM1,905,990 vs RM2,496,170 budget (-24%), RM1,546/ha vs RM1,953, including depreciation and amortisation RM801,018 (RM650/ha vs RM783). Excluding depreciation the subtotals add to RM896/ha vs RM1,169 (consistent with RM76/t). Staff salary and related RM339,727 vs RM533,140 (salary 211,814; welfare 109,975 vs 215,504). Labour overhead RM416,550 vs RM537,352 (wages 222,419; SOCSO/EIS 114,593; recruitment 43,870). Office and related RM307,343 vs RM366,240 (land and building tax 99,889; vehicle allocation 76,205; water/electricity 43,110; security 25,280 vs 98,881; telecom 18,375 unbudgeted). Other building maintenance RM34,701 vs RM19,105, over budget because of under-budgeted repairs to workers' quarters and a tube well for the quarters' water supply.

UPKEEP AND CULTIVATION (PA, 12 months to April 2026, mature 1,117 ha, FFB 14,472 t): total RM3,208,538 vs RM2,934,819 (+9%); RM2,527/ha vs RM2,488. Lines RM (RM/ha): supervision 48,554 (43 vs 52); selective spraying 43,675 (39 vs 76); circle/access 68,809 (62 vs 66); manuring labour 58,368 (52); fertiliser material 1,297,543 (1,162 vs 1,568); composting 387,266 vs 156,759 (no RM/ha shown); pests and diseases 18,132 (16); census and thinning 10,709 (10 vs 8); drains 276,738 (248 vs 178, +39%); roads and bridges 815,388 vs 268,888 (730 vs 241, +203%); soil and water conservation 28,604 (26 vs 45); supplying 39,506 (35 vs 46); pruning 81,388 (73 vs 113); tools 5,108 (5 vs 21). Roads overspent because part of capital expenditure was charged to operating cost on Head Office instruction.

WEEDING (PA): circle spraying 2,518.59 ha (2.3 rounds), 68% of 3,698 ha budgeted (3 rounds); deferred where circles were clean; circles generally clean. CKS high-volume output only 2.5 ha/man-day (potential 5), sprayers refill from field drains; piece rate RM14.44/ha, earnings RM36.10/day vs basic RM33.54; glyphosate 0.45 L/ha plus Ally 0.02 kg/ha (PA advises dropping Ally); 18 pumps a day, about 6 hours worked. Advice: VLV low-volume nozzles, jerry cans of pre-mixed water from the chemical store, one circle round converted to raking where needed. Selective spraying 2,285.37 ha (2 rounds), 62% of 3,698 ha (the printed total leaves out Division 1B); CKS and knapsack power sprayer both 3 ha/man-day, earnings RM43.22; paraquat 0.45 L/ha plus Ally 0.02 (CKS) or 0.01 kg/ha (power sprayer). Interrows well under control.
Agronomy 1/2026: two rounds of circle and selective spraying completed in FY 2025/26, third round only in part. Woody weeds much reduced, but sparse or bare ground from over-spraying/blanket spraying is now dominant (SR23-13C1, SR23-13A1, SR22-13A1, SO23-19A1, SO14-23B1, SP14-13B1) and soft grass cover has fallen. Bracken ferns (Stenochlaena palustris, Dicranopteris linearis) in low-lying areas; encourage Nephrolepis (as at UMB SEP Estate). Mist blowers: one-sided covers in sparse areas, round covers in tall grass.

MANURING: PA says 226 of 261 rounds budgeted were applied, delayed by late Mix B delivery; broadcast, 20 bags/man-day, RM2.05/bag, earnings RM41/day; recommends rubber-track Badang semi-mechanical application. Compost 85.03 of 590.2 ha budgeted (labour shortage), 20 t/ha, 50 palms/man-day at RM1.08/palm (earnings RM54). Agro Table 4 FY 2025/26 (kg): Mix B 949,900 programmed, 728,820 applied (77%); ZA 132,980 of 139,050 (96%); RP 261,700 of 282,450 (93%); compound 10,200 of 18,050 (57%); MOP 25,200 (100%); kieserite 10,500 of 12,600 (83%); HGFB 850 of 840 (101%); urea 8,200 of 8,400 (98%); plus extras Hi-Kay 40,550, NK Mix 53,800 (carried forward from FY24/25), NPK 15.15.6.4 13,850, NPK 12.12.7.2 4,050, Kaptan 600. Total applied 1,291,300 of 1,436,490 programmed (90%); delivered 1,215,410. Straight fertiliser in SP14-13A1 and SP14-13B1 is a trial. Estate says the programme was effectively completed and records look short because rehab blocks have varying palm stands; re-census to fix requirements. Completion was 60% in FY2023/24 and 80% in FY2024/25. Caked fertiliser must be broken before use. Agro Table 6 compost: 2,995 t on 149.77 ha (57% of 261.15 ha targeted) at 20 t/ha; compost looks barely more decomposed than raw EFB, so compare C/N and review cost. Frond stacking recommended but only localised; Belida Estate is the reference.
FERTILISER PROGRAMME FY 2026/27 (Agro Appendix 1a, kg/palm): P2013, P2014, P2015: Mix B 2.20 Jun, RP 1.70 Jul, Mix B 2.20 Sep, Mix B 2.00 Jan, Mix B 2.00 Mar. P2017-P2019: Mix B 2.20 May, RP 2.00 Jul, Mix B 2.20 Aug, Mix B 2.00 Nov, Mix B 2.00 Feb. P2020: Mix B 1.70 four rounds plus RP 2.00. Immature (Appendix 1b, titled 2025/2026): P2022 Mix B 1.50 x4 + RP 2.00; P2023 compound 1.50 x2, RP 2.00, Mix B 1.50 x2; P2024 compound 1.20, RP 1.70, compound 1.20, 1.50, 1.50; P2025 600 g CRF + 500 g RP in hole, then Com A 0.80 (month 9), RP 1.50 (month 10), Com A 0.90 (month 12). No manuring for blocks to be replanted in 2027. Programme must not run past April 2027.
LEAF AND SOIL (Agro): most fields deficient in N and P, localised K shortfall, Mg sufficient to high; key constraints low P and marginal N, addressed in the FY2026/27 programme. Leaf samples Oct 2025: N 1.97-2.77%, P 0.124-0.176%. Palm vigour much improved since late 2025.

PEST AND DISEASE: PA says rat census below the 10% threshold so no baiting, but its table shows SP13-13A1 fresh damage 21.40% and SM21-13A2 14.81%. Agro Table 10 (Nov-Dec 2025, Mar 2026): estate total old damage 3.53%, fresh 5.07% on 31,678 palms sampled; Div 1A 1.88%/2.92%, Div 2 4.45%/6.27%; SO14-13B1 old 11.43%. Estate baited in January 2026 where fresh damage exceeded its 5% action level, replacing bait every 3-4 days; Agro wants a second-generation bait lasting 7 days. Fresh damage now minimal. No other pests or diseases seen (PA).

ROADS, DRAINS, WATER (PA): grading 3,694 m vs 3,217 m budget, RM42.01/m vs RM46.72. Lateriting 13,178 of 15,000 m (capex), RM54.57/m vs RM68.17 budget. Roads very satisfactory. Desilting 54,032 m, RM256,293 vs RM198,698; unit costs above budget (RM2.02-3.83/m). All 6 budgeted water gates installed (RM7,218 each).

PRUNING: PA 92,600 of 103,476 palms (89%), 648 ha; Div 1A 93%, 1B 89%, 2 87%, 3 91%; text 1,295.10 of 1,447.22 ha; 120 ha of rehab areas left unpruned while recovering; cost RM0.79/palm/year; poor pruning quality in SM07/19A1. Agro Table 8: progressive 2,979.80 of 3,698.40 ha (81%), corrective 330 of 285.74 ha (115%). Pruning generally satisfactory; slight over-pruning of shorter palms persists (SO14-23B1, SO12-17C1); half-length frond cutting much reduced; keep two subtending fronds; sanitation pruning in SO12-17C1 commended.

HARVESTING (PA, 12 months to April 2026): RM965,117 vs RM1,014,543 = RM66.69/t vs RM64.43 budget. Supervision RM3.83/t (6.73; one checker fewer), harvesting own FFB RM34.24 (38.27; rates cut as bunch weights rose, reviewed every 6 months), infield loading RM5.85 (6.29), internal transport RM22.00 (12.32; estate's own tractors used more than contract), tools RM0.76 (0.83). Rounds 33 vs 36 budgeted to April. Interval averaged 15 days at the visit, deliberately lengthened in dry weather; outturn 96%. Harvester output (t/man-day, RM/day; basic RM33): 2013 1.69, 42.63; 2014 1.62, 40.44; 2015 1.41, 49.18; 2017 0.90, 31.66; 2018 1.03, 36.15 (rate RM25/t for 2013-14, RM35/t later). 2017/2018 harvesters work only about 6 hours. Crawler trial for infield evacuation (5 cutters + 2 collectors) reached 3.5 t/man-day.
Agro: intervals 9-21 days, average 14, target 10 days; Table 7 Div 1B 15-21 days, Jan-Apr 2026 longer (harvester shortage). Rounds achievement 86% in FY2025/26 vs 78% in FY2024/25 (target 3 rounds a month; Agro says 2 good rounds are enough). SO12-17C1 15% over-ripe at platform; heavy losses from over-ripe and rotten bunches and uncollected loose fruit in SO12-17C1 and SN14-18B1 (2 of 16 sub-blocks, 12.5%), worse away from main roads.

CROP (PA section 8, May 2025 to April 2026, 1,116.66 ha): FFB 14,472.34 t vs budget 15,746.12 t (-8%); last year same period 12,217.66 t; FY24/25 annual budget 18,272.42 t. Yield 12.96 t/ha vs 14.10 budget and 10.94 last year (+18%). CPO 2,921.96 t, OER 20.19% vs 21.50% budget (18.89% last year); kernel 479.03 t, KER 3.31% (3.60%). CPO yield 2.62 t/ha vs 3.03. Shortfall blamed on harvester shortage; bunch formation still low in rehabilitated blocks. Bunch weight April 2025 to April 2026: 2013 12.2 to 14.2 kg; 2014 12.1 to 15.0; 2015 9.6 to 12.5; 2017 8.0 to 9.1; 2018 7.8 to 8.3; 2019 7.8 to 8.0.
Agro yield history (Table 11, FFB t/ha by financial year May-April): 2018/19 3.54, 2019/20 6.51, 2020/21 8.75, 2021/22 9.99, 2022/23 10.89, 2023/24 11.44, 2024/25 10.91, 2025/26 12.97. Near-plateau 10.89-12.97 since 2022/23 due to past rehab issues. Average bunch weight 9.71 kg FY24/25 to 11.14 kg FY25/26. Table 12 (1,115.68 ha) shows FY25/26 12.97 vs 10.91 (+19%). Best sub-blocks FY25/26: SO14-13A1 37.84, SO12-13A1 34.70, SN13-13A1 32.31, SP13-13A1 31.55, SM18-13A1 31.09. Table 12 prints FY2024/25 and FY2025/26 yields for 103 mature sub-blocks (1,115.68 ha); the dashboard block list uses these printed sub-block figures directly (SPH per sub-block from PA Appendix 1). Weakest: SP15-14B1 and SN07-19A1 nil both years, SN09-19A1 0.13, SS23-17B1 0.96, SS23-17D1 1.18, SS22-17A1 1.33, SS21-17A1 1.47 (rehabilitated 2017 blocks). Biggest falls: SO21-19A1 -25%, SN14-13A1 -25%, SM18-13A1 -23%, SO13-13A1 -23%, SN13-13A1 -20%. Neither report prints a yield per planting year, so none is shown.

COST OF PRODUCTION (PA section 9, 12 months to April 2026, RM per tonne FFB): general charges 76 vs 95; upkeep and cultivation 222 vs 186; harvesting and collection 67 vs 64; total ex-estate 365 vs 346. Total per tonne CPO RM1,807 vs 1,647. High cost from overspending on upkeep (roads, drains, composting) and harvesting/collection (internal transport). The RM/t general charges match the general charges excluding depreciation (calculated).

VEHICLES (PA): Triton jeep RM19,152 for 35,125 km, RM0.55/km vs 1.04. Tractors RM455,209 for 17,765 hrs, RM25.62/hr vs 16.84, high repair costs.
CAPEX (PA): access road laterite RM925,557 vs RM1,245,604 (main road 429,074; collection road 496,483); buildings budget RM373,299 (barrack RM262,016 not spent); crawler dumper RM52,755 vs RM211,020. Underspent overall.

IMMATURE AND NURSERY: PA: SN14/24B1 (2.45 ha) and SN14/25B1 (7.64 ha) circle and selective spraying 100%, manuring not yet due, sporadic rat damage, supply seedlings still in nursery. Agro: SM10-24A1 planting acceptable but vacant points remain. New pre-nursery with good traceability; cull abnormal seedlings; flush irrigation pipes fortnightly; replace fortnightly Mancozeb-only spraying with a weekly rotation (triazoles, strobilurins) against Curvularia.

RAINFALL (Agro Table 5): 2020 2,753 mm/148 days; 2021 3,161/173; 2022 2,582/180; 2023 3,097/134; 2024 3,159/195; 2025 3,299/175; average 3,009 mm, 14 rain days a month, 17.9 mm per rain day. PA Appendix 3 (no year headings) gives 3,279.25 mm/156 days for the 2025 column.

DATA CAVEATS: yield 12.96 (PA) vs 12.97 (Agro); Agro text +7.24% should be about +19%; planted area 1,246.27 / 1,244.63 / 1,233 ha.
